| FX → Premiere text: layer name and generator range | The writer rejects a text layer whose name (written as `InstanceName`) contains a character that XML 1.0 cannot represent, and one whose source out-point (one hour into the graphic generator plus its duration) passes the signed 64-bit tick range. Such a layer fails the whole export, in check mode as in execution, rather than being omitted; names have no length limit. | No lossy conversion: the export fails instead of panicking or writing invalid XML. Synthetic model tests and a public check/execution test of a control-character name; names with escaped delimiters, Unicode and line breaks still round-trip. |
| Either direction: clip effect stack order and bypass | Standard (non-intrinsic) clip effects keep their Premiere stack order, the order in which they apply. Premiere renders a `VideoComponentChain` in descending component `Index`: Index 0 renders last, and any materialized Motion/Opacity sit at the lowest Indexes and apply after the effects. Import reads that order and export writes the FX order reversed, through one mapping (`schema::chain_render_order`); component `ID`s record creation order and do not order the render. **Before** (JRB-1954, #4370), both directions read Index 0 as the first-applied effect, so every stack of two or more effects, a Crop or wipe included, was reversed; **after** (F24), stacks follow Premiere's render order. Omission messages count stack positions in that order (1 is the first effect applied); against main, the corpus shows such message changes in 14 chains of 4 cases, with the same omissions, and 3 two-blur chains, in 3 cases, whose converted order changes. Premiere `Bypass` `true` ↔ FX `enabled: false`; a disabled FX effect keeps its slot, id and values. Import constructs ordered `fx_schema::EffectRecord`s directly on the video layer, with `enabled` from bypass; export writes the current FX order, reversed into the chain as above, and enabled state (a pre-v13 effect without an id exports as enabled), with component IDs from 4 and the Motion writer's record generation (`VideoFilterComponent` 7, `Component` 5, parameters 9). Both writer choices are inferred: corpus chains start their standard effects at ID 3 (140 chains) or 4 (76), and every corpus blur is `VideoFilterComponent` 8 with `Component` 6. Native component IDs, record versions and Effect Controls UI state are not kept. An omitted effect is simply absent, so later effects then run on the output of earlier ones. Component `Index` values that disagree with document order omit that chain's effects as ambiguous. Supported intrinsic Opacity keeps its existing path; Vector Motion (`AE.ADBE Graphic Group`) next to effects still omits the whole occurrence, and standard effects on the sequence (`VideoTrackGroup`) chain still reject the sequence. | **No new loss** in order and bypass of mapped effects. Synthetic reader, writer and mapper tests (hand-written edited stacks export; written stacks reload through the reader), chain-shape rejection tests, and an edit, export and reimport of the derived fixture `premiere_isolated_effect_stack` (structural only; its AME video was a diagnostic mismatch while the Tint was omitted, its blur edges matching Tesseract's active-blur-only render at 0.9920–0.9987; the Tint converts since JRB-2001, see the Tint row). **Adobe evidence** for the order (Oracle run C6): in the F1 render (`premiere_isolated_stage_order_26_5`) a Crop or wipe at Index 0 with a blur at Index 1 renders sharp, so the blur applies first, and the swapped layout soft; a probe that swapped the component `ID`s rendered the same frames; the F1m render has Motion at Index 0 applying after the effects. Premiere reopens exported chains in the written Index order (gate G1, next row). Named F24 flips, expectations from those facts: `static_blur_stack_keeps_order_values_and_bypass`, `unknown_effect_between_blurs_keeps_the_remaining_stack_order`, `keying_and_masked_effects_omit_their_occurrence` (the `horror_title` Track Matte Key is now stack position 1), `converted_effects_keep_their_side_of_a_crop_or_wipe`, `converted_effects_on_both_sides_of_a_crop_are_omitted` (omission order), `omitted_effects_do_not_count_on_either_side_of_a_crop`, `flat_crop_or_linear_wipe_exports_its_effects_after_it`, `adobe_derived_stack_imports_order_bypass_and_its_tint` (until JRB-2001 `…_and_reports_the_unknown_effect`) and `edited_stack_order_and_bypass_survive_export_and_reimport`; slice 4's (JRB-2004) `adobe_keyed_blurs_import_as_editable_blurriness_tracks` (clip B of `premiere_isolated_gaussian_blur_keys_26_5` imports as [blur 10 (id 2), keyed (id 3)]; before, the reverse) and, test only with supervisor approval, `imported_blurriness_keys_target_schema_params_and_evaluate_as_premiere` in `crates/fx_composition/tests/premiere_keyframes.rs` (B's keys are on effect id 3; before, 2); slice 8's (JRB-2008) `corner_pin_keeps_its_bypass_and_order_around_a_blur` (a Corner Pin at Index 0 and a blur at Index 1 import as [blur, Corner Pin]; before, in Index order). |
| Either direction: Crop or Linear Wipe with standard effects (JRB-2077) | Converted effects keep their side of one non-default active Crop or supported cardinal Linear Wipe. Premiere applies the standard effects in stack order (descending chain `Index`, previous row), Crop and wipe among them, then Motion and Opacity; FX applies a layer's masks before its effects. Effects that apply after the mask stay on the one video layer; effects that apply before it stage the clip (next row). Export writes a flat clip's effects at lower Indexes than its Crop or wipe and a stage group's at higher Indexes. Converted effects on **both** sides of the mask are **omitted and reported** with `MASK_EFFECT_ORDER_REASON`, and the clip keeps its mask; an unmapped or failed effect is omitted where it stands and counts on neither side. A Crop at neutral values is no mask. A bypassed native Crop or wipe keeps its clip and is reported without applying the effect. Unsupported active wipes omit the occurrence through the existing wipe reader. Keyed Blurriness (JRB-2004) and a Corner Pin (JRB-2008) with its corner keys convert the same way, their keys on the video's effect (the stage group's video when staged), counted from the source In. **Before** (`main`), a blur or Corner Pin beside an active Crop or wipe was omitted with `MASK_EFFECT_ORDER_REASON`. | **Adobe evidence** for the order: Oracle run C6's F1 render (previous row). The F1 and F1m saves are the manifest cases `premiere_isolated_stage_order_26_5` and `premiere_isolated_stage_motion_26_5` (`structural_only`), which main omits clip by clip; their public tests check each clip's import, the written chains in the saved Index order and the reimport (`adobe_stage_order_fixture_converts_each_order_and_writes_it_back`, `adobe_stage_motion_fixture_keeps_motion_masks_and_keys_and_writes_them_back`). Their AME renders are pinned as long-term Assets (`LE8eVJuhbr3RDFRcej9D_vid`, `GHOw7TwGAH6i1EL7XBuS_vid`), and fresh conversions scored against them at the standard floors are below them (means 0.931972 and 0.964604), lowest on the blurred clips: each clip has AME's sharp or soft edge class, so the order holds in both renders, but the soft edges are narrower than AME's, so blur width is **unverified**. Export is verified in Adobe (Oracle gate G1, candidate `2bfe36b45`): Premiere 26.5.1 reopens the export of F1 with A's Crop Top and B's Blurriness edited, and of F1m unedited, with no warning, each chain in the written Index order and each value and key as written (25 readbacks within 2.6e-6); AME renders the reopened F1m RGB-identical to its C6 render in 240 of 240 frames, and F1 as edited (`export_gate` in each `comparison.json`). A blur beside uniform Scale, Rotation and Position renders with FX Blurriness times the mean world scale (renderer code); a blur with non-uniform Scale and Crop Edge Feather stay approximate. **Unbounded** visual error for effects omitted on both sides. The Corner Pin's place against the mask is **inferred** from renderer code (no render measured), and no imported corpus chain has one. Tests: `converted_effects_keep_their_side_of_a_crop_or_wipe`, `converted_effects_on_both_sides_of_a_crop_are_omitted`, `flat_crop_or_linear_wipe_exports_its_effects_after_it`, `keyed_effects_before_or_after_a_crop_or_keyed_wipe_import_export_and_reread` and `moved_linear_wipe_stages_and_omits_the_effects_below_it`; they replace main's `crop_with_blur_keeps_geometry_and_reports_unrepresentable_stack_order`, `linear_wipe_with_blur_keeps_geometry_and_reports_unrepresentable_stack_order`, `crop_exports_without_guessing_its_order_against_blur` and `linear_wipe_exports_without_guessing_its_order_against_blur`. |
| Either direction: clip processing stage (JRB-2077) | A Crop or wipe that applies after a converted effect (at a lower chain `Index` than it), or a wipe on a clip with nondefault static Motion or Motion keys, imports as a `GroupLayer` ("Premiere stage N") that takes the clip's range, Enable, Motion, Opacity, their keys and the mask. The video under it keeps its id, source range, playback, frame blending and the effects that apply before the mask; it and the guide are at the identity in the video's frame, on the group clock. A Directional Blur among those effects is **omitted and reported** in both directions ("a Directional Blur on a stage group is not converted"): no Adobe case verifies its map through the group's Motion (follow-up). A Crop with no converted effect applied before it stays one video layer whose guide repeats the video's static transform and Position, Rotation and Scale keys: before, keyed Rotation or Scale dropped the Crop and showed the cropped content. Guide and mask ids are as for a flat clip, and the group takes the next id, so each staged clip shifts later ids by one. Export writes a stage group (one mask whose guide is one of its two children, the other a video) as **one clip**: Motion, Opacity, keys, Enable, range and the mask from the group; media, ranges, playback, frame blending and effects from the video; those effects apply before the mask and are written at higher chain Indexes than it. A video whose range is not the group's still omits the group whole with 6a's reason: its nest would outlast its content, which rejects the whole export. A group without that shape keeps the nest rules. Mask Opacity is a linear coverage scale (G1, alpha 0.500257 fitted). Inverted at Mask Opacity 100 is exact (G3b: the fixture's inverted clip D renders Mask Opacity × (1 − coverage)). **Approximation, reported once per clip in both directions** (`Mask Feather converts one to one to the FX mask feather, an approximation; Premiere feather visuals are not preserved exactly`, D17-2): Premiere's feather at Feather 60 is a Gaussian edge of σ ≈ 22.1 px on the source frame (G2: 10–90 % width 56.6 px), a fit of σ ≈ 0.368 × Feather at that one radius, not a measured all-radius law; the FX mask feather is the renderer's separable 9-tap blur (`crates/renderer/src/backend/render_pass/compositing/masks_opacity.rs:1988–2005`, `text_shadow_blur_params` and `compute_blur_params` in `backend/mod.rs:479–489` and `443–457` with divisor 2.4, one base iteration and a 4 px step cap, `shaders/blur.wgsl`), whose effective σ is about 0.7 × Feather up to 9.6 px and above that about 6.8 × √min(64, ⌈(Feather / 9.6)²⌉) px: the iteration count is `ceil(base × (Feather / 9.6)²)` capped by `iterations.min(64)` (`mod.rs:456`), and the base of 1 stays 1 in both the Export and the Playback quality mode (`QualityMode::scale_blur_iterations`, `types.rs:194–199`), so σ grows with Feather up to about 77 px and stays near 54 px above that (Feather 1000 renders σ ≈ 54 px, not ≈ 708) — no parameter transform makes the two equal, so Feather passes one to one and a Feather-60 edge renders about twice as wide as Premiere's. The export gate measures the gap at Feather 20 (the 10–90 % width of C's inverted ellipse edge): native **18.443 / 18.777 px** (σ ≈ 7.2–7.3 px, again ≈ 0.36 × Feather, a second radius of the fit) against FX **38.036 / 38.088 px** (σ ≈ 14.85 px; the formula above gives 6.8 × √5 ≈ 15.2 px), so Mask Feather is not preserved exactly in either direction. The 26.5.1 form's exact vertex geometry along a Bézier segment is unmeasured (G3: the only crossing straight segment is within 0.75 px). | Either direction: Track Matte Key (`AE.ADBE Legacy Key Track Matte`, JRB-2023) | Premiere keys a media or nest placement's source frame by one channel of another video track's output at the same sequence time, applied at the key's chain position like a Crop (standard effects at higher `Index` first, then the key, then Motion and Opacity), and does not draw the matte clip while it is consumed (**measured**, fixture G1: the matte region shows the fill with mean error 1.03 levels and no white matte rectangle; G1b: clip D's matte hid while its key was one this converter rejected, matte region → base 0.53 levels). So an active key consumes its matte track over the keyed clip's range whether or not the clip converts: the reader records every active key's matte track and range before it reads the placement (`claimed_matte_track_ids`, `consume_claimed_mattes`), and a matte clip over an omitted clip's range that no kept clip keys is **omitted** with the clip's record (`matte source of the omitted clip <record> was not converted: Premiere does not draw a track-matte source`); a clip omitted while it converted (a stage rule, a timing rule) drops its unconsumed matte layer the same way (`omit_unconsumed_mattes`). **Output change**: before, a rejected clip left its matte visible (the Oracle's candidate run left clip D's matte a root image with no consumer). The matte converts only when its track is strictly above the placement's and holds exactly one enabled item or nest spanning exactly the placement's range (D13-4: a superset or partial matte fails closed, because Premiere shows a matte clip outside the placement's range and FX never does), without a Crop, wipe, Opacity mask or key of its own; it converts as its ordinary layer (video, still, Color Matte rectangle, graphic or nest), its Blend Mode included, by **inference** (how Premiere's key reads a blended matte clip is unmeasured), and the FX `trackMatte` names that root layer. A clip at default static Motion with no converted effect applied before the key keeps the key on its own video or nest group (flat: FX evaluates a sibling source at its own canvas position, as Premiere keys the sequence-sized frame); any other clip stages (clip processing stage row) and the matte moves under the stage group as its direct child on the group clock, so the group's Motion moves the matte with the video, as Premiere's Motion moves the keyed picture (G5); effects applied before the key stay on the staged video (G7). | FX → Premiere: a Crop or wipe that cannot export omits its clip (F17) | One check (`canonical_mask`) decides every mask of a video clip (a still exports none; see the still image row), and it reads every authored key, including keys that export otherwise omits. The clip **omits whole** ("masks cannot be exported: …; occurrence omitted") unless its mask is one plain Add mask without keys whose guide is one plain rectangle beside the video, and: the guide's shape, not its name, selects the check: a black sequence-sized rectangle whose only animation is one ScaleX or ScaleY key track is a wipe guide, unless a flat guide's video has that track too (a flat Crop guide repeats its video's keys, and the feathered Crop of a whole sequence-sized frame has that rectangle); any other guide is a Crop guide. A Crop guide has square corners and exactly its video's transform and transform keys or, unkeyed beside an unkeyed video with both only translating (2D, Scale 100, Rotation 0, no skew or 3D rotation), another anchor and position, which move its rectangle into the video's frame by the difference of their offsets (position minus anchor); a translated rectangle outside the frame omits the clip with the Crop's validation reason, and a translated guide beside equal non-identity Scale, Rotation or keys stays out of scope and omits the clip; a wipe guide is a cardinal wipe, anchored and positioned at the frame edge that its angle names, with one Scale track of keys and no other key; a flat wipe's video maps its frame onto the canvas unchanged (anchor at the position, Scale 100, Rotation 0, no skew or 3D, sequence-sized, and no key on a transform property). The guide and its keys are consumed and the other clips export. Neither the picture nor the sound of the omitted clip is inspected, so its unreadable media no longer rejects the export; an audio layer's asset is always inspected. Its own keys are reported with the generic lines: layer keys with "animation on an omitted or unsupported layer was not exported", its effects' keys with "unsupported animation target was not exported". Before, such a clip exported **without its mask**, showing what the mask hides (on #4465 `main`, a flat wipe on a moved or keyed clip stopped the export instead: Motion row). Two flat cases now omit the clip where `main` and r1 exported it with its Crop or wipe and reported only the dropped animation. **Before**, a video key on a frame property that export drops and the Crop guide does not repeat (for example Anchor Point keys; on a wipe's video, any such key) exported the clip; **after**, the clip is omitted whole. **Before**, a video animation on a frame property that is not one enabled, independent keyframe track (for example disabled keys or a script) exported the clip; **after**, the clip is omitted whole. A flat Crop guide now also needs what a wipe guide needed (not hidden, Normal blend, no matte, masks, effects or motion blur). A stage group whose mask this check rejects is omitted whole (clip processing stage row). A Crop or wipe that exports is not reported: **before**, every exported flat Crop also reported "masks was not exported" on its video. **Before (EX2a)**, the name `Premiere Linear Wipe guide N` selected the wipe check: a renamed wipe guide omitted its clip with the Crop reason ("the Crop guide's transform or Motion keys differ from its video's"), a Crop guide with that name with "the Linear Wipe guide is not a black sequence-sized rectangle", and a Crop guide that only translated from its video with the Crop reason; **after**, each exports its Crop or wipe. A wipe guide with a second track now fails the Crop checks instead of "the Linear Wipe guide needs exactly one Scale track"; import writes neither such guide nor any guide that changes its check. | No successful lossy conversion. Synthetic tests: an inverted Crop, a wipe guide with a second track (Crop reason), a canonical wipe on a Scale 80 video, a Crop guide without its video's keys, a rounded Crop guide, a moved wipe guide, and keys on a Crop guide's roundness, on a wipe guide beside its Scale track, on the video's Anchor Point and on the mask, and a disabled Rotation animation on the video of a Crop and of a wipe, and a translated Crop guide beside equal Scale 80, equal Rotation 10 or equal Position keys, or off the frame, each with a kept sibling; exports of a renamed wipe guide, a wipe-named Crop guide, the feathered whole-frame Crop whose guide repeats its video's one Scale track, and a guide and video translated by distinct anchors and positions (`mask_guides_export_as_the_crop_or_linear_wipe_that_their_shape_draws`). A public export of an inverted flat Crop and an inverted stage group, each audible and playing media that is not a video, keeps the sibling and an audio layer's own sound (`media_of_a_clip_omitted_for_its_mask_is_not_inspected`). Named flips: `unsupported_crop_mask_omits_its_video_and_keeps_siblings` (public round trip) and `crop_mask_rectangles_are_guides_not_authored_mattes_in_either_layer_order` (an inverted Crop on the only clip now rejects the export and lists the omission); EX2a: the second-track and roundness-key wipe rows of `masks_that_one_crop_or_wipe_cannot_carry_omit_their_clip_or_stage`, flat and staged, now give the Crop reasons. |

| FX → Premiere: a flip, or a Scale or Rotation outside Motion's bounds, omits its clip | Premiere's Motion holds Scale 0 to 10000 and Rotation −32768 to 32767 (`MOTION_PARAMS`). "flip (negative scale) was not exported" for any negative Scale, otherwise "static scale …", "Scale animation …", "static rotation …" or "Rotation animation exceeds Premiere's supported range". Its media is not inspected; a stage group falls to the nest path, whose placement is omitted for the same reason ("group was not exported as a nested sequence: …"). **Before**, a static value outside the bounds exported as Scale 100 or Rotation 0 ("… (using 100%)", "… (using 0 degrees)"), so a flip was lost and the size or angle changed, and keys outside them, or whose easing left them between two keys, were written as they were. | No lossy conversion. | Premiere → FX: Corner Pin (`AE.ADBE Corner Pin`, JRB-2008) | Upper Left, Upper Right, Lower Left and Lower Right ↔ FX `cornerPin` `upperLeftX` … `lowerRightY` unchanged: Premiere and FX both normalize each corner to the clip's own frame (origin top left, y down), and off-frame values stay. A keyed corner becomes two FX `effectProperty` tracks, its x and y, with the point keys' times, values and temporal easing, through the Motion point-key reader and the effect-key rules of the keyed-blur row: times move to the layer clock and keys outside the trim stay; Linear `0` and Hold `4` stay; Bezier `5` keeps Motion's mapping from the speeds along the spatial path, which on a straight path is each coordinate's progress. A keyed corner's `StartKeyframe` is ignored and its static FX value is its first key's. Key ids are `premiere-effect-{effect id}-{paramName}-{layer id}-{index}`. Only straight spatial paths convert (supervisor decision D-8a): each spatial control point (a key plus its tangent) must lie within 1e-9 of the clip frame of the segment to the next key. On a straight path a corner moves by its temporal easing alone, so the separate x and y tracks reproduce it and the tangents themselves are not kept; Premiere writes float noise into the automatic tangents of straight paths (1.4e-17 in the corpus spin of `mixkit-41`). A quad that is degenerate or non-convex at any time omits the effect, because no perspective warp maps the clip frame onto it and Premiere's rendering of one is unmeasured: at the static corners and at any corner key, where each other corner takes its value at that time (D-8b), and between two consecutive key times of any corner, where each corner stays on one point or moves along its straight segment (a supervisor decision). Between keys the check is exact when every moving corner is Linear or all share one Bezier easing between the same keys, because each turn of the quad is then a quadratic in one parameter. Otherwise it bounds each corner's eased progress on its own, overshoot included, and requires a strictly convex quad at every combination of these bounds, which is sound but conservative. Each turn must be nonzero with one sign in f64, with no tolerance. | Values are exact; key times round to at most **0.5 ms** on import, as for the keyed blur. An accepted spatial control point is off the straight path by at most 1e-9 of the frame (below 1e-5 px at 8K). Oracle run E1 measured Premiere's Corner Pin as a perspective (projective) warp with corners normalized to the clip's own frame, as FX draws it (renderer code reading); the antialiased edge band is not modelled. Synthetic reader tests (Premiere 26.5.1 records, the corpus `mixkit-41` spin with automatic tangents, curved-path and quad rejections), mapper tests, the Premiere 26.5.1 fixture `premiere_isolated_corner_pin` (static skew, off-frame corners, Linear and Hold keys from a nonzero In, a two-corner spin, the identity quad; import test), and the `premiere_keyframes.rs` guard: every keyed corner is an animatable `cornerPin` parameter, and FX evaluates the fixture's keys within 1e-12 of the values Premiere 26.5.1 reads back. The case stays `structural_only` and unscored: a diagnostic score of the fixture's Tesseract render against its pinned AME reference measured a mean of 0.970052 and a minimum of 0.951742 (standard floors 0.99 and 0.98), with the differences on edges only, the identity quad included: a renderer gap in edge antialiasing (cause not verified). |

Synthetic tests of each host form in both directions, of the range in the clip's frame, of Film Impact's record and of a staged blur beside a flat one (`directional_blur_on_a_stage_group_is_omitted_on_import`, `…_on_export`), and of a blur on a nest placement, inside a nest that is rotated or has Rotation, Scale or Position keys, and inside a plain nest or one with only Opacity keys (`a_directional_blur_that_a_nest_placement_would_map_is_omitted`). |

Chromatic Aberration must be zero. Edge Behavior 2, the Legacy blur's transparent exterior, and Premiere's default 1 (repeated edges) import as the same blur; 0 (mirrored edges), keys on other controls and hidden values other than Premiere 26.5.1's defaults omit only the effect with a reason. Edge Behavior 1 is approximate: FX `directionalBlur` keeps the transparent exterior, so a round trip writes Edge Behavior 2. At Amount 35 the two differ by more than 1/255 only within about 130 px of the frame edges that the blur crosses, a band that widens with Amount, and Edge Behavior 1 differs from Legacy Blur Length 55 by a mean of 4.8/255. Angle has Legacy Direction's axis and sign: probe axes of 0°, 90° and 26° for Angle 0, 90 and 30, against 18° for Direction 30 and 161° for Direction −30. Seed 1234 leaves the blur unchanged (mean difference 0.15/255); Chromatic Aberration 50 does not (6.1). Kernels, colour processing and edge behavior differ as for the Legacy blur (Directional Blur row). Resolution scaling is unmeasured. The probe fixture's chart is QTRLE, which the media importer rejects, so parser/mapping tests do not establish a fresh-import visual result; the case is `structural_only`. |

Amount 1000 is Blur Length 1600 in the clip's frame, above Legacy's 1000, so export writes no Legacy records and accepts up to 1600; a longer static value or key omits the effect ("blurLength … is outside Premiere's 0 to 1600 range", the FX value in the clip's frame). Host, stage, nest and key rules are the Directional Blur row's. | **Approximation** (current Premiere row). An exported value reimports unchanged to f64 rounding. **Export is Adobe-verified** on one document of six one-second chart clips (evidence folder above): Premiere 26.5.1 reopens it and reads back every Angle, Amount and Edge Behavior 2, Motion Scale 50 and Rotation 30 on the scaled clip, and the Amount (Hold, then Linear) and Angle keys at clip times 0.3, 0.65 and 0.9 s. The UI was not inspected, and one resolution was tested. |
| Either direction: Levels (`PR.ADBE Levels`, master) ↔ FX `levels` (JRB-2000) | The master (RGB) row maps: Black Input, White Input, Black Output and White Output Level ↔ `inputBlack`, `inputWhite`, `outputBlack` and `outputWhite` unchanged (0–255), and Gamma ↔ `gamma` = native / 100 (native 0–1000). Keyed master levels become `effectProperty` tracks through the keyed-blur key path (layer-clock times, keys outside the trim kept, Linear, Hold and Bezier; key ids `premiere-effect-{effect id}-{paramName}-{layer id}-{index}`), and a keyed level's static FX value is its first key's. The (R), (G) and (B) rows must be neutral (0, 255, 0, 255, 100) and unkeyed (supervisor decision D11b-1). Premiere 26.5.1 saves Levels as a Premiere-native filter (Oracle run E4): `VideoFilterType` 1, no `Bypass` or `Intrinsic`, every `ParameterID` −1, so parameters are identified by `Name`, and a `PremiereFilterPrivateData` of the 20 `StartKeyframe` values as little-endian u16s, which import requires (40 bytes, equal), reading an empty copy's blob by its `BinaryHash`. Export rounds each FX value and key to a whole level and Gamma to hundredths (D11b-2) and writes the Premiere 26.5.1 form: `VideoFilterComponent` 9 with `Component` 7, no flags, `ParameterID` −1, neutral (R), (G) and (B) rows, static `IsTimeVarying` `false` (inferred), a keyed level with `IsTimeVarying` `true` as E4 saves it and its first key as `StartKeyframe` (inferred) and the private data of the written `StartKeyframe`s under a random `BinaryHash` (inferred). | Import values are exact, and whole native values export unchanged. Export rounding moves a value or key by at most **0.5 level** or **0.005 gamma**; between keys the curve follows the rounded keys with the same easing, and a cubic Bézier between two keys that round to one value is written flat. Key times as for the keyed blur (at most 0.5 ms on import, exact milliseconds on export). **Measured, not scored** (AME, Oracle run E4, 22 frames of `premiere_isolated_levels`): Premiere applies Levels to clipped 8-bit encoded values with the exponent 1/(Gamma/100), and the FX formula fits every frame at a mean \|d\| of 0.18–0.62 levels (p99 ≤ 1.9), where linear-light and inverted-gamma models miss by 7–42 levels; D's Linear ramp follows within encoder noise, its Hold holds and its last key is exact at 8.000 s. That measures the formula, not a Tesseract render. Scored against that render, now pinned, a fresh conversion measures a mean of 0.979926 and a minimum of 0.972670, below the standard floors: the difference lies on the source's edges and texture in every clip, including the near-identity one (a renderer gap), so the case stays `structural_only`. Unrendered: per-channel rows, black input with Gamma together and keys on other levels. **Export passed a scoped Adobe gate** (`export_gate` in `premiere_isolated_levels`): Premiere 26.5.1 reopened an edited export and kept every value, flag and key, and AME rendered it; the record form, the `BinaryHash`, static `IsTimeVarying` `false` and a keyed level's `StartKeyframe` are inferred from the corpus and were accepted by that reopen. Synthetic reader, writer and mapper tests, the Premiere 26.5.1 fixture `premiere_isolated_levels` (import, and edit, export and reimport) and the `premiere_keyframes.rs` guard (every bound name is an animatable `levels` parameter; D's ramp evaluates to Premiere's rounded readback, 196 and 192). |
| Either direction: unsupported Levels variants (JRB-2000) | Import: a non-neutral or keyed (R), (G) or (B) row; private data that is missing, not base64, of another length or unequal to a `StartKeyframe`; a parameter `Name` missing, repeated or unknown; a `ParameterID` other than −1; `Bypass` or `Intrinsic` present (a bypassed Premiere-native filter is unverified, D11b-4); a static level outside its range. Export: a value or key outside Premiere's range (levels 0–255, gamma 0–10) or non-finite, never clamped; a disabled FX Levels (D11b-4). Both directions: input black at or above input white, or output black above output white, at any time (D11b-5), for the FX values and keys and for their rounded export; keyed levels are bounded by their keys, Bézier overshoot included. The cause is **inferred**, a renderer gap (**not compensated**, supervisor decision D11a-2): E3 fits Premiere's effect per channel on encoded BT.709 values as o = m·(v − 128.8) + 128.8 + B·max(1, m) with m = 1 + C/100 for C ≤ 0 and 1/(1 − C/100) above (a measured fit, rms 0.01–0.94 levels in 22 AME frames; the decoding without clipping is inferred, and the pivot and gain are fitted on 5 parameter sets), while FX draws libpag's After Effects approximation (contrast 1 + C/300, value × (1 + B/250 or B/650) plus an offset); applied to the source frames, that formula **predicts** mean differences of 4 to 22 levels from AME (A 21.7–22.0, B 4.8–6.2, C 8.9–9.0, D 4.1–10.3), a prediction, not a Tesseract measurement. Synthetic reader (Premiere 26.5.1 records and a corpus 12.1 record with a Bezier Brightness pulse and Linear Contrast keys), writer and mapper tests; the Premiere 26.5.1 fixture `premiere_isolated_brightness_contrast` (import, and edit, export and reimport; `structural_only`); the `premiere_keyframes.rs` guard, where FX evaluates D's keys at the values Premiere 26.5.1 reads back. Export is Adobe-verified on one edited document: 215 record checks with 0 differences, a reopen in Premiere 26.5.1 without a warning that reads all 6 sampled values back exactly, and an AME render as edited, the bypassed clip unchanged (evidence in `premiere_isolated_brightness_contrast`). The corpus's 68 records sit on nests, adjustment layers, a Color Matte and an image sequence, so none converts. |

They match by a prefix of their match name or English display name, so a wrong entry only omits more clips: Radial Wipe by its observed match name (`AE.ADBE Radial Wipe`, 2 corpus records), legacy keyers other than Track Matte Key by the prefix `AE.ADBE Legacy Key ` inferred from its match name (an active Track Matte Key is the clip's mask, Track Matte Key row, JRB-2023; a bypassed one is reported like any bypassed keyer), and Ultra Key, Luma Key, Chroma Key, Color Key, Image Matte Key, Difference Matte, Non Red Key and Alpha Adjust by English display name because their match names are unobserved; a localized project keeps such a clip and reports the keyer as an unknown effect. An effect whose `SubComponents` reference an `AE.ADBE AEMask` mask also counts (JRB-2028). A clip with a static Opacity mask (such a reference on its Opacity) converts (static Opacity mask row, JRB-2028); a **bypassed** masked effect that has a mapping is omitted as a feature for its `SubComponents` (the effect reader's child list), not imported as a disabled effect without its mask. A **bypassed** one (`Bypass` `true`) keeps its clip, which Premiere renders without it, and is reported as an unknown bypassed effect; a missing `Bypass` reads as active (inferred) and an invalid one counts as active. Other Transition video effects, such as Gradient Wipe, are unobserved and not in the rule: they keep the clip and are reported as unknown effects. Wipe transitions between clips belong to JRB-1985. | **Unbounded** visual error (the clip is missing, or for an effect outside the rule the clip stays opaque). | Premiere → FX caption cues | Each cue becomes a root FX text layer (a cue with a background box, a group of one text: next row); wording (CR paragraph breaks become LF), timeline range, PostScript font, size, fill, stroke and shadow are kept as for single-style text, the stroke and shadow through the text stroke and static text shadow rows. Each cue is read from its own `FormattedTextData`; its track's `CaptionDataTemplateStyle` is only decoded, so that an unknown caption layout omits the track. Converted caption cues extend the document end like converted audio; AME behaviour for a caption past the last video item is unverified (JRB-1964 probes covered video items only). Placement is Adobe-verified (the pinned AME render's 1- and 2-line 48 px cues and 4 calibration renders: 72 px, TitleSafe 10/10, ActionSafe 20/20, 720×1280): the default-position corpus cues store no geometry, only center justification and bottom alignment, and Premiere draws such a cue with its last line's baseline at 95% of the frame height, centred, and earlier lines 120% of the size apart, whatever the size, the line count and the project's title-safe and action-safe margins. A cue becomes bottom-aligned box text whose lowest line slot has its baseline at 95% of the frame height (y 1026 on 1080p), pivoted on that baseline at the frame's centre; the box holds the whole line slots above it plus half a slot, so any line count up to its capacity ends on that baseline. A centred cue's box is 80% of the frame wide; a left-justified cue's box spans the middle 75% (x 240–1680 on 1920), **INFERRED** from two AME renders: one left cue's ink started at x 242 and one right cue's ended at x 1677. The wrap width is unverified: no fixture cue wraps. Paint order is Adobe-verified: every visible caption track is drawn above the video and the higher track Index paints over a lower one where cues overlap (the `premiere_isolated_caption_styles_26_5` render: of 777 pixels that C1's white and C7's stroke or shadow share, the overlap frame shows 0 white). Cues on a hidden track and disabled cues (`IsMuted=true`) import as hidden layers, read as for video clips (inferred). Right or full justification, another alignment, a text box, all caps, tracking, leading, a disabled fill and a stored position (document slot 33, whose sub-slots AME renders measured as linear frame offsets but whose base point is unknown) omit the cue by name: no render covers them. Caption track grouping survives only as layer names (`C1 caption 2`) and paint order; the language code, the generator in-point and a disabled shadow's stored values are not retained. | Placement residual **at most 0.65 px** over the calibration renders of `premiere_isolated_captions` derivatives (each changes one parameter; AME 2026 build 85, default Match Source preset): the last baseline measures y 1026.32–1026.65 on 1920×1080 for 48 and 72 px and one or two lines (rule: 1026), and 1216.03–1216.07 on 720×1280 (rule: 1216; a fixed 54 px margin would give 1226); TitleSafe 20→10 and ActionSafe 10→20 renders decode frame-for-frame identical (framemd5) to the default render (Asset `05KEehDuFJtJcKAyanY1_vid`). Caption size is verified on 1920×1080 only: Premiere scales caption size with the frame (the 48 px cues drew at 2/3 size on 720×1280, which is 720/1080 = 1280/1920, so a short-side, long-side or area rule cannot be told apart from 16:9 and 9:16 renders). On another canvas each cue keeps its stored size and its sequence reports one approximation (`caption size on a W×H canvas`); caption size scaling is part of JRB-1958. Rendered in Tesseract, the glyphs sit about 1.6 px above Adobe's: the model baseline is exactly 1026.0, and the renderer's glyph snapping of box text draws the flat-stem edge at about y 1024.8 against Adobe's 1026.4 (a renderer follow-up; the same render shifted 1.6 px down scores 0.9994448 mean / 0.9980063 minimum). Widths, line breaks and centring match; both caption tracks render, and overlapping cues are superimposed at one position in both. The resolution rule is Adobe-verified on fill and background only (a cue's own green fill draws green; a cue without a background over a background template draws no box; cues with their own background draw it); the other attributes follow the same rule by inference. The left margin is inferred from two cues, so a wrong margin moves a left cue by that error. Record shapes come from Premiere 25.5 (project Version 43: the 42 cues of the 29.97 fps `Practice-Sequence_CAPTIONS-ONLY` in `practice_files_transcription_magic`, whose template and cues share one style; manifest case `practice_files_transcription_magic_captions_only`) and from Premiere 26.5.1 (the `premiere_isolated_caption_styles_26_5` fixture's 7 cues and 3 templates, whose payloads use the same markers and which Premiere 26.5.1 saved byte-identical). Another caption layout omits every cue (fail-closed). No corpus caption track or cue is muted. No AME render shows a shadowed or stroked *corpus* caption; the fixture render draws the stroke and shadow template (C7, C3) and the fill override (C2); the pinned comparison scores 0.9961–0.9979 on those cues' samples (C7 0.9978, C2 0.9976, C3 0.9961), a measured distance, not a pass (caption background box row). Evidence: synthetic XML tests, the real corpus cue and template payloads, the `premiere_isolated_captions` fixture (25.5 caption records spliced into a 26.3 scaffold), whose AME comparison (0.9982659 mean / 0.9948304 minimum) stays a diagnostic mismatch (`structural_only`) until that renderer offset is fixed, and the `premiere_isolated_caption_styles_26_5` fixture (Premiere 26.5.1 save; its AME render is pinned and the fresh comparison measures 0.996546 mean / 0.987420 minimum, `structural_only`: the standard policy passes with each tested cue removed); see `tests/README.md`. |
| Either direction: caption background box | A caption cue's enabled Background (document slots 17 color, 18 on, 19 opacity, 20 size, 34 corner radius) ↔ an FX group of the cue's one text layer whose group background is the box: `size` px of padding on every side, one solid fill of the color, and `radius` px on every corner. **Adobe-verified at 48 px** (two AME calibration renders with five boxes on "TILE", plus the fixture's C4): the box is one rectangle around the whole block, not per line (a two-line cue: one 236.7 × 111.1 px box), sized (ink width + 2·size) × (ink height + 2·size) and centred on the ink within 0.4 px (size 10 → 120.0 × 52.6 px, size 30 → 160.0 × 92.7 px), with all four corners rounded by the radius (15 fits a circle within 0.23 px; C4's 15 fits 14.75). Export writes every background slot and the calibrated form only: a group of one 48 px text with equal paddings, one opaque solid normal-blend fill and equal radii, whose text layer is unscaled, unrotated, opaque and without keys (the FX renderer pads its group box in group space whatever the child's transform and opacity; Premiere draws the box as part of the transformed text object, so a scaled, rotated, translucent or keyed child is unverified, while the child's anchor and position are a pure translation at scale 100 and rotation 0, which both engines carry box and text through alike, and the group's own Vector Motion scales or rotates both together); any other group background is omitted as a feature and the text exports (a group of several objects with a background is still omitted whole). **Fail-closed, background omitted and text kept:** a text size other than 48 (scaling of the size with the text is unverified), an opacity other than 100 (only 100 was rendered; the shadow calibration showed that Premiere blends opacity in linear light, so another value is not a linear alpha), a radius above half the box's least height (Premiere's clamp is unknown: at radius 30 on a 52.6 px box the ends fit 27.5 px, nearly semicircular, at 0.95 px rmse), and a payload that omits one of the five slots (a Premiere 25.5 caption style preview omits opacity and radius; the default Premiere draws then is unverified). The least box height is **INFERRED**: 2·size + 24 px (about an x-height; the renders measured 32.6 px of ink for 48 px capitals) + 57.6 px per further line (the measured 1.2 × 48 line pitch), so a lowercase- or punctuation-only line can be shorter and a radius between the true and this half height stays unverified. A Type-tool graphic's background is still omitted as a feature: no render calibrated one (follow-up). | Box geometry residual ≤ 0.4 px in size and centre and ≤ 0.23 px in radius over the 6 rendered boxes at 48 px; other sizes, opacities and clamped radii are not converted. The FX renderer derives the group box from the laid-out text content, not the glyph ink: its box is 78 px tall against Premiere's 54 px around the same ink (measured on the pinned render at 6.75 s), which is not compensated. The fresh Premiere/FX render comparison measures mean 0.996546 / minimum 0.987420 (standard 0.99/0.98 at 0.25 s), but the policy passes with each tested cue removed (C2, C4, C1) and fails an empty render by 0.00003 only, so it cannot prove the cues; no justified case-specific policy is established and the case stays `structural_only` with no `score_policy`. Evidence: the Premiere 26.5.1 save of `premiere_isolated_caption_styles_26_5` (C4: color (0,0,255), opacity 100, size 10, radius 15, whose payload this crate's encoder reproduces byte for byte), its AME render (box 440.4 × 54.7 px around 422 × 36 px of ink; pinned as a long-term reference), the two calibration renders that contain the five box cases (`oracle/12/calibration.md`), payload, reader and conversion tests in both directions, and the fixture's public round trip, which keeps every cue and reports C4's exported box on the Type-tool reimport. The export gate (an edited export reopened in Premiere 26.5.1 and rendered by AME) passed structurally: the box is written and drawn (440.51 × 54.83 px, radius 15.00, against the fixture's 440.4 × 54.7, 14.75), but every exported cue, box and text, renders 28.3 px lower than the caption fixture: the pre-existing bottom-aligned box export (the box text first line row's line-slot estimate) places the pivot on the FX renderer's slot baseline 28.4 px above the box bottom, and the shift fits Premiere putting a bottom-aligned Type-tool box's last baseline at the box bottom (INFERRED from the measured offset, one render; correction uncalibrated, follow-up). |
| Premiere → FX caption styling, placement and structure | No lossy conversion: a cue with a disabled fill, all caps, tracking or leading, right or full justification, another alignment, a text box, a stored position, several text blocks, effects, retiming or playback speed is omitted and its reason names the attribute; an unverified background is omitted as a feature by name and the text converts (the caption background box row). Overlapping, repeated and dangling cues on one track, a repeated track Index, malformed `IsMuted` values or template styles, and tracks beyond the remaining share of the sequence's 256 timed items (including audio) also omit. The rest of the timeline converts. | Synthetic XML tests assert the exact reason for every styling and placement attribute, that a cue converts from its own payload under a template of another font, size or shadow, and each structural omission listed here, including the real corpus cue and template; not every malformed-record path has its own test. Adobe evidence for the resolution rule and the background is in the rows above; none for the other omitted attributes. |
| FX → Premiere caption-derived text | Caption layers export as Type-tool box graphics through the single-style text writer, not as caption tracks: caption tracks, their language and template style are not recreated, and a reimport reads graphics. | The FX → Premiere text rows apply (f32 narrowing, box first-line and line-slot differences). | Either direction: sequence canvas of another size (custom canvas) | Import keeps a sequence's `FrameRect` as the document size: 1920×1080 or any other positive size at the origin (portrait, 720p, square, ultrawide, odd). Each placement's `FrameRect` must be the frame of the sequence that holds it, whatever its source's size (the Adobe-saved `vhsvertical` places a 1080p nest in its portrait sequence with the portrait frame); a placement with another frame is omitted alone. Media and stills keep their natural size: Anchor Point × the source size, Position × the canvas, nothing rescaled; graphic object positions are fractions of the canvas. A Color Matte or adjustment layer imports over the whole canvas, so one whose stream is another size omits its placement; a nest of another canvas is omitted (the nested placement rows). Export writes the document size as the sequence, placement and generator frames and each video keeps its source frame; an automatic AEP scope and its link take the same canvas (Automatic Premiere + AE packages). A zero, negative, overflowing, off-origin or malformed frame, or non-square sequence, placement or stream pixels, omit that sequence or placement. Premiere saves square pixels as `PixelAspectRatio` `1,1` or as any equal pair (`1280,1280`, `1920,1920`, `1000000,1000000`). An unequal pair, such as HDV `1920,1440` or DVCPRO HD `3,2`, is non-square, and its diagnostic names the ratio; a malformed pair is rejected. Captions keep their stored size (caption cues row). | Geometry is **exact** by construction (no rounding or implicit scale). **Video reference (root canvas):** the Premiere 26.5.1 save `premiere_isolated_vertical_canvas_26_5` scores 0.99264 / 0.98756 against its AME render under the standard policy (`premiere_26_5_vertical_canvas_imports_at_its_own_size` checks its editable import). The export of the derived `premiere_isolated_vertical_canvas` import reopened in Premiere 26.5.1, and its AME render equals the native render at all 60 frames; at frame 0 both renders exceed the declared 3.0-level colour bound against the source media (4.0), a recorded failure accepted by a user exception for this root proof only ([fixture index](../../tests/README.md#vertical-canvas-premiere-2651-video-reference)). **Structural only:** the derived fixture in both directions (`derived_vertical_canvas_converts_at_its_own_size_in_both_directions`; its own pinned render is not compared) and synthetic canvas, matte, adjustment, graphic, nest, caption, staging, export-loss and AEP-scope/linked-import tests; no Adobe open or render of a custom-size Premiere + AE package was run. **Open:** caption size (caption cues row) and a nest or generator of another size. Script-baked normalized points fit to half a pixel of the longer side of their clip's frame (script animation row). |
| Premiere → FX timing: clip boundaries | Absolute native start and end ticks round to the nearest millisecond, ties forward, at all eight supported sequence rates. Each duration is the rounded end minus the rounded start, so adjacent clips stay adjacent. Keyframes stay clip-relative. The document does not keep the native sequence frame rate. Unlisted sequence rates are omitted, not approximated; retimed clips follow the retiming row. | At most **0.5 ms per boundary**; a duration changes by less than 1 ms, and a key moves at most 1 ms on the timeline. Synthetic rounding tests at every supported rate and synthetic mapper tests. Manual check: projects saved by Premiere Pro 26.5.1 at every supported rate converted again, and Chrome played the converted documents. |
| Premiere → FX timing: source range | Source-in rounds to the nearest millisecond, ties forward. At unit speed the source duration is the rounded active duration, so the clip keeps normal speed, and the native source-out is not kept separately; a forward unit-speed `OutPoint` up to one sequence frame from In plus the duration is stale and reads as that played end (synthetic structural tests; no Adobe-native fixture in this repository), while a larger difference still rejects; a constant-speed or reversed clip keeps its rounded source span, and a time-remapped clip spans the whole media (retiming row). | Source-in moves at most **0.5 ms**. Identical source-frame selection is **not** guaranteed. The rounded clip start and source-in, and the renderer's whole-millisecond frame times, can make a source request up to 1.5 ms early. That is past the decoder's 0.499 ms guard, so a frame can show the previous source frame (**at most one**). Measured against the pinned 24 fps Adobe cut: 12 of the next clip's 35 frames show the previous frame; the mixed-rate case matches on all 48 frames. |
| FX → Premiere timing: clip boundaries and document duration | Export writes a 30 fps sequence, or the rate that `--fps` selects; an imported sequence rate is not restored. Absolute layer start and end and a video-determined document duration snap to the nearest frame of the sequence rate, ties forward. Keyframes keep their clip-relative times and move with the clip. A layer that snaps to zero frames **rejects**. | At most **half a frame (16.7 ms at 30 fps) per boundary**; a duration changes by less than one frame (33.3 ms at 30 fps). Synthetic snapping, adjacency, and collapse tests. Manual Premiere Pro 26.5.1 reopen/render: every exported sequence was exactly 30 fps, shared cuts stayed shared, and a 200 ms gap rendered as six black frames. At `--fps 24` the Adobe-derived `premiere_isolated_rate_24_cut` returns to its frame-37 cut (CLI test); AME 26.5 renders of a probe at 23.976, 24, 25, 29.97 and 59.94 fps have the written rate, frame count and cuts. |
| FX → Premiere: sequence rate (`--fps`) | Each rate writes the `MZ.Sequence.VideoTimeDisplayFormat` of its Adobe-saved corpus sequences: 110, 100, 101, 102 (drop-frame), 104 and 106 for 23.976, 24, 25, 29.97, 30 and 59.94 fps. 50 and 60 fps **reject**: no corpus sequence pins their code. Stills, Color Mattes and graphics start one hour into their synthetic clock, rounded down to a frame. Generator clips keep `DefaultIsDropFrame` `false` and all media log `TimecodeFormat` 104 at every rate. | The display code, drop-frame preference and logging format set only Premiere's timecode display, and the reader ignores them; Adobe-saved generators use the drop-frame state and code of their rate. Codes from 196 Adobe-saved sequences in 26 corpus projects (`format/writer/sequence.rs`); generator in-points from Adobe-saved projects at 24, 25, 29.97 and 30 fps, inferred at 23.976 and 59.94 fps. AME 26.5 renders at those five rates show the text, Color Matte and still on their first and last frames. Premiere's timecode display was not inspected. |
| FX → Premiere timing: source range | Source-in stays exact in milliseconds and does not snap. At unit speed, source-out is source-in plus the snapped duration. A layer whose source duration differs from its active duration without `playback` or `source.timeRemap` **rejects** as a retime; retimed layers follow the retiming row. | Source-in is exact; the source duration changes with the snapped duration, by less than **one sequence frame (33.3 ms at 30 fps)**. Synthetic tests; Premiere 26.5.1 readback kept source-in at 0.117 and 0.464 s. |
| Either direction: video constant speed, reverse and Time Remapping | Import: a native constant `PlaybackSpeed` and `PlayBackwards` become two linear FX playback keys over the rounded active range, from the rounded source start to its end (reversed for reverse); a Time Remapping ramp becomes one FX playback key per native key (times rounded to milliseconds, easing as for Motion keys) on the full media clock, and a ramp that FX cannot hold omits its occurrence. Export: a two-key linear playback spanning the source range, forward or reversed, writes native speed and reverse at rate = exact source span / frame-snapped active span; any other playback curve, and a `source.timeRemap` whose source range is longer or shorter than the active range, **omits its occurrence** ("time remapping was not exported: Premiere export writes constant speed only"); **before**, both exported at unit speed in a source window that fits the media (`playback was not exported`, `time remap source duration was approximated with a constant-speed window`, or a shifted source start), so other frames played. FX does not render `source.timeRemap`, so one over an equal span exports at unit speed with a report. Intrinsic keys on a retimed clip: import omits Motion and Opacity keys as **features** when the native rate is not 1 (the reader already omits a time-remapped clip with Motion or Opacity keys); export omits them when the rate it writes is not 1. Static values, a static Crop and the clip stay. A Linear Wipe on such a clip omits its **occurrence** on import, as on a time-remapped clip, and stops the export. Effect parameter keys are outside this rule, and their timing on a retimed clip is not verified. Sound of a retimed video is reported and not exported (audio rows). | Import rate error comes only from the millisecond boundary rounding (≤0.5 ms per boundary). The exported rate differs from the FX rate by the relative change of the frame snap (under one sequence frame over the clip duration). A playback that native speed cannot carry is omitted, not approximated (no lossy conversion; `playback_that_native_speed_cannot_carry_omits_its_clip`). Key omission is fail-closed: no Adobe fixture pins Premiere's key clock under speed, reverse or remapping. Evidence: `premiere_isolated_variable_speed_ramp` passes its AME comparison (`video_reference`, forward positive ramp only); `premiere_isolated_constant_reverse_0_905` stays `structural_only` (only pre-fix scores exist); synthetic keyed and cropped edits of the pinned reverse fixture, a speed edit of the pinned wipe fixture and synthetic export documents cover the key rules. |
| Either direction: media frame rate and duration | Packaging keeps original media bytes and physical timing independent of sequence rate; `sourceIntrinsicDuration` uses whole milliseconds. Listed Constant import checks exact native/file rate and duration. Unlisted Constant, quantized and irregular import use the separately documented count/endpoint checks and approximation warnings; irregular additionally requires unit playback. Export **rejects** non-Constant/unlisted clocks and mismatched packaged duration. | **No new loss** in media records; `sourceIntrinsicDuration` rounds by at most **0.5 ms**. Six-frame media at all eight rates, a mixed-rate integration test, and synthetic mismatch tests; Premiere 26.5.1 readback kept each source's own rate at speed 1, and conversion did not change the source bytes. |
| Either direction: final-frame hold at the media end | Source-out can pass the exact media end by one sequence frame plus 1.5 ms, so the last source image holds for the final frame. Conversion does not clamp the clip or change the asset duration. Synthetic reader, writer, and mapper tests; a Premiere 26.5.1 render of a rounding-only tail showed the source on all 31 clip frames. |
| Either direction: MP4/MOV edit-list movie duration | A full-source edit list can store the ceiling of the exact sample duration in the movie time base, or end inside the last frame: the iPhone capture `IMG_2439.mov` edits 1208/600 s of 49 frames of 25/600 s, and Premiere 26.5.1 saves all 49 frames as its `Duration` (`518616000000`; HDR row). The media duration always comes from the exact sample timeline, so such a tail is kept as Premiere keeps it. | A longer segment differs by **less than one movie tick and one source frame**, a shorter one by **less than one source frame**; for example, a 250.25 ms source accepts 209 to 251 ms, and a segment exactly one frame short rejects. Tests mutate real fixtures; a probe of the real capture, with its display matrix set to identity in memory, reads the saved `Duration`. |
| Premiere → FX audio levels | Static source, clip, track, and master Volume and Mute, the clip's Clip Gain (`AudioClip/Gain`, linear) and its intrinsic clip Volume multiply into one linear layer volume; which stage held each level is lost. The clip Volume `Level` maps by layout: `[Mute, Level]` (Premiere 25/26, project versions 43 and 45) plays 0 dB at 0.177827939391 (10^(−15/20)), `[Bypass, Level]` (up to Premiere 13, versions 25–36) at 0.5; a Level without a value is 1.0 (+15 dB, +6.02 dB) and 0 is silence. A clip with Enable off (`ClipTrackItem/IsMuted`), a clip Volume with Mute on, a track with its timeline mute on (`Track/IsMuted`), or a muted master imports at volume 0. A Channel Volume at its layout's unity (0.177827939391 or 0.5 on every channel) converts; any other value or key on it is reported (`clip Channel Volume not converted`) and the clip plays without it. A bypassed clip Volume, an invalid value, a parameter that cannot be read or parameters in neither layout (before: reported as other audio processing), Bezier Level keys (none in the corpus), or Level keys under a disabled `IsTimeVarying` are reported (`clip Volume not converted: …`) and the clip plays without its Volume, as before. Level keys follow the next row. Track, master and source automation and keyed Mute (static value used), pan, solo, insert effects and transitions are reported and dropped; a track that lists any audio transition reports one Feature omission (`audio transitions not converted`), and its placements keep their own levels without the fade. A placement with remapped channels or a non-mono/stereo layout is omitted. The older `{"SerializerWrappedObject":[…]}` channel layout reads as its array, and a Premiere 26.5.1 mono chain without `AudioChannelLayout` as the default. A broken or ambiguous link to one audio track or placement omits only that member; a broken panner, transition or master-track link still omits its whole track or audio group. | Levels are **exact**. AME renders ([clip Volume evidence](../../tests/README.md#evidence-contract)): the Premiere 26.5.1 `premiere_isolated_audio_volume_levels` measures every stereo static Level within 0.002 dB of 20·log10(v/0.177827939), Clip Gain included (+15 dB on its unclipped samples), the 0.5 fader within 0.004 dB, mono 3.01 dB lower (see the mono row); `premiere_isolated_audio_volume_legacy` (an XML derivative of a Premiere 10.4 save) within 0.018 dB of 20·log10(2v); `premiere_isolated_audio_clips` matches its 0.5 track fader within 0.001 dB. Historical Tesseract renders of the converted cases are within 0.11 dB of AME per static segment (0.070 dB levels, 0.106 dB legacy), apart from mono (+3.00 dB) and the omitted Channel Volume (+6.01 dB). Synthetic tests cover the fail-closed variants. That `IsMuted` silences an audio track is inferred from the field name; no corpus project has a muted or soloed audio track. Transitions and link recovery have structural reader tests only: synthetic edits of the pinned audio cases, including the Adobe-saved Constant Power `AudioTransitionTrackItem:1078` copied from `adobe_native_variable_speed_ramp_ppro25`; no Adobe render of an audio transition. |
| Premiere → FX clip Volume keys | The `Level` keys (the 8-field Motion shape, on the source clock) become one editable FX `AudioVolume` key track on the audio layer: one key per native key at layer time key − In (millisecond rounding as for Motion; keys before In and after Out are kept), valued at the Level gain times every other static stage. Hold stays Hold. Premiere moves a Linear segment linearly in a fader position u, u = g^0.4475 up to 0 dB and 2 − g^−0.4475 above (AME renders), not in gain or dB. Each Linear segment therefore gets a CubicBezier easing with x1 = 1/3, x2 = 2/3 (a cubic in time) and y1, y2 in [0, 1] (the gain stays between the keys), fitted to the curve between that segment's own native Level values: a deterministic golden-section minimax of the dB error above −60 dB. The native handles (influence 1/6 and speeds) and the playhead `CurrentValue` are not kept; the value before the keys stays the layer volume. A key track that cannot import, such as one with two keys that round to the same millisecond, is reported once (`volume animation was not imported: …; the sound was kept at zero gain`), and the audio layer keeps its placement and source at zero gain, for root and nested sounds alike; before, it kept the value before the keys, which played through their silent intervals. | Values at the keys are **exact**. Between keys, against the u-model: at most **0.241 dB** when both keys are within −60…+3 dB (0 ↔ −20 dB: 0.002 dB); fades from or to silence at most **1.18 dB** when the other key is at +6 dB or lower; up to **4.03 dB** (−60 ↔ +15 dB) and **4.27 dB** (silence ↔ +15 dB) above that. No corpus key is above 0 dB. Plain linear gain would err 3.0 dB (0 ↔ −20 dB). The mixer samples the curve at 100 Hz, so a Hold step lands within 5 ms. Historical Tesseract renders against AME: a 1 s fade midpoint 0.005 dB apart; an 80 ms fade −0.38 to +0.42 dB and a silence → +6 dB fade −1.11 to +0.85 dB, mostly the fitted easing's own error (`model_delta_db`); the Hold step 4.9 ms early; the linked A/V fade 0.23 dB quieter because Tesseract keeps the source's 1024 unsignaled AAC priming samples (21.3 ms) that Premiere drops; the legacy key pair −0.015 to +0.035 dB. The name replaces only the demuxer's rejection: a file it reads is admitted as before, and a RIFF header, whose little-endian size spells `ftyp` at 0x70797466 bytes, keeps the demuxer's verdict. Adobe proof does not cover mono key interpolation, noncenter or other routes, transport playback or Effect Controls. CPU tests cover static/keyed gain, Clip Gain, stereo controls, unsupported routing and nested no-double normalization. |

The pinned audio case keeps its 1 s offset and 0.25 s trim exactly; synthetic tests cover rounding, the allowance, and the retimed and reversed omissions. |
| FX → Premiere audio levels and volume keys | Each sound placement writes its level in the native clip Volume, as Premiere 26.5.1 does (`[Mute, Level]`: `Internal Volume Stereo` with `Internal Channel Volume Stereo` at unity, or `Internal Volume Mono`); every track fader stays at unity, where the level used to go. A static Level holds at most +15 dB; Clip Gain takes the rest. `AudioVolume` keys become Level keys on the source clock (In plus layer time), each at its FX time and value. Hold stays Hold. A segment whose easing is the fit that import gives Premiere's Linear segment between the Levels that export writes stays one Linear segment; for mono sound, export predicts those Levels without the centered mono factor, up to 3.01 dB below them. Any other segment (FX Linear, another CubicBezier, a fit of Levels above 0 dB, or a fit that goes below −60 dB and whose Levels export or that mono prediction rescales, such as a fade from silence) becomes Linear pieces whose added keys lie on the FX curve and on the layer's 1 ms grid, until each piece is within 0.25 dB of the FX curve wherever the FX gain is at or above −60 dB, or is 1 ms long; such a 1 ms piece is kept and reported once per track (`volume curve approximated, not removed: …`). A track with a gain that is not finite and nonnegative is reported (`volume animation was not exported: …`) and its sound keeps its static volume; an embedded sound that is then silent is omitted and reported. When the loudest key or the static value of a keyed volume is above 0 dB, that peak becomes Clip Gain and every written Level is at or below 0 dB, so Premiere shows the boost as Clip Gain rather than as Level. An audible video layer with volume keys exports its sound even without a static volume; volume keys inside a group are reported with the group's sound. | Values at the keys are **exact** (a Level rounds by at most one unit in the last place). Between keys, against the u-model: a recognized fit writes its native segment back, so Premiere plays the native curve, which differs from the FX easing by the import fit's error (previous row); every other piece is within **0.25 dB** of the FX curve above −60 dB. That check is sampled (64 curve parameters, the curve's turning points and each −60 dB crossing), not a proof for arbitrary cubics; tests confirm it on independent denser grids. A reported 1 ms piece is **not bounded**: 0.29 dB at 1–2 ms of a 1 s fade from silence; 24.69 dB in the first millisecond (below −40 dB) of a 100 ms fade from silence. Reimport fits each written piece again and adds that fit's error (≤ 0.25 dB between audible keys, ≤ 1.5 dB from or to silence; 1.09 dB measured). A written piece whose Levels stay below −60 dB reimports as FX Linear, so where Clip Gain lifts it above −60 dB, exporting the reimported project again can add keys there: the keys case's fade from silence to +6 dB gains one (layer time −394 ms, −59.8 dB) on its second export and none on the third to fifth. A 1 s FX Linear fade from silence to 0 dB writes 12 inner keys and reimports at 0.489 halfway (one Linear segment gave 0.192). Synthetic export-model, writer read-back, mapper and public round-trip tests cover Volume curves; the quota cleanup adds 4097-key preservation instead of the former refusal. Historical export gate of candidate `4ba9eb383`, which wrote every non-Hold easing as one Linear segment (`export_gate` in the levels and keys evidence): Premiere 26.5.1 reopened both exports with no warning text and read back all 11 static Levels and 12 keys, the Hold as a Hold (UI not inspected); AME renders the levels export sample-identical to the original project's render and the keys export within 0.003 dB of it, except the two fades whose native curve crosses 0 dB (80 ms −20 → +6 dB: up to +1.29 dB; silence → +6 dB: up to +1.43 dB). The historical export at `77c4f5b58` writes the same levels records, and the same keys records apart from those two fades, which get three inner Linear keys each (normalized byte comparison). Centered-mono normalization changes the current writer's mono records; that historical comparison does not establish its new output (the centered mono row has the static mono export evidence). An AME render of the revised export (`export.fixture_export` in the keys evidence) plays those pieces within 0.024 dB of the u-model and 0.268 dB of the FX curve (6 windows at the 80 ms fade's steep start exceed 0.25 dB), where the old export measured 1.47 and 2.27 dB; a diagnostic fit attributes the excess to Premiere applying the clip Volume about 2 samples late. Its authored 1 s fade from silence is measured only in its first 10 ms, where the source has signal: −0.52 dB from the FX curve in the 1–2 ms piece, then −0.25 to −0.16 dB; its midpoint is unmeasured there. On a continuous tone (`export.authored_fade` in the keys evidence), the same fade plays its midpoint −0.20 dB from the FX curve (within 0.25 dB at every candidate alignment) and −0.004 dB from the written curve, but not the whole fade within 0.25 dB: 25 of 199 windows exceed it, up to 0.48 dB in the first 10 ms, which depend on the alignment. An AME render of a static +20 dB export (Level 1.0 plus Clip Gain 1.778; `export.static_plus20db`) plays within 0.001 dB of +20 dB. The converter at `77c4f5b58` exports the same native projects for these three renders after normalization. |
| FX → Premiere audio timing and embedded sound | A hidden video's picture exports disabled, but its embedded sound is reported and omitted because whether the FX renderer mutes hidden layers is unverified. Sound boundaries stay exact in milliseconds and do not snap to the sequence grid; the picture of an audible video layer snaps. Its sound is a separate, unlinked placement of the same Media. The sound of a video layer with `playback` or a source time remap is reported and not exported; its picture keeps its native constant speed or reverse. An audio layer whose `playback` makes its source duration differ from its active duration is omitted as an occurrence (`retimed audio layer was not exported`) and the export continues; a duration-preserving `playback` exports at unit speed with a `playback settings` report; without `playback` the mismatch rejects. | The picture can move up to **half a frame (16.7 ms at 30 fps)** relative to its own sound. Synthetic mapper and relocation tests. The retimed audio layer has a public export test (`retimed_audio_layer_is_omitted_while_picture_and_other_sound_export`), and a mapper test covers the duration-preserving `playback` and the duration mismatch (`audio_playback_keeping_its_duration_exports_while_a_mismatched_retime_rejects`); no Adobe evidence for it. AME 2026 build 85 rendered three generated projects before export moved levels into the clip Volume: the audio-clips round trip is sample-exact against the source mix (fader 0.5 at −0.001 dB); a five-track document with mono, 2.0, 0.25, and 0 levels is exact per placement; the linked A/V round trip renders an AAC stream byte-identical to the Adobe-authored project's render and 150/150 identical frames. Premiere Pro's UI was not inspected. |
| FX → Premiere: source sound that cannot be exported | More than two channels, non-AAC MP4/MOV audio (for example PCM `sowt`), an unsupported or retimed edit list, and malformed audio no longer fail the export: an audio layer is omitted as an **occurrence**, and the embedded sound of an audible video as a **feature** (`embedded audio was not exported: …`) while its picture exports. A failed read, or an archive entry whose bytes fail their source hash, still stops the export, even when its sound is unsupported. On import, a failed media read also stops the batch; only a WAV or MP3 stream that ends early is omitted as malformed. | The dropped sound is lost (**unbounded**), and reported. Synthetic public tests: FFmpeg-made MP4 with its `mp4a` entry renamed to `sowt`, a WAV patched to six channels, a tampered archive payload, and failing readers; no pinned native source has an unsupported sound stream. No Adobe evidence. |
| Either direction: sound-only MP4/MOV media | Import packages each file with its container's asset kind (MP4/MOV Video, M4A/WAV/MP3 Audio, PNG/JPEG Image). A sound-only MP4 or MOV, such as the audio-only Media that export writes for an audio layer of an A/V MP4, is therefore a Video asset, and export admits it again; before, import packaged it as Audio and the next export rejected it. | **No new loss**: bytes, `video/mp4` content type and SHA-256 unchanged. Structural evidence only: two export/import cycles of a synthetic edit of the pinned `feature_linked_av_strict` case. No Adobe proof that Premiere or AME accepts an audio-only Media for an A/V MP4; no audio-only MP4/MOV fixture exists. |

FX excludes hidden layers from mattes, so JRB-2023 must import a Premiere matte source visible. A span covered only by disabled clips is treated as a gap (black canvas). **Behaviour change:** disabled clips (and clips on an output-off track) count toward the sequence end, so one ending after the last enabled clip, previously omitted, now lengthens the document; AME renders black there (derived fixture below). Pixels cannot tell a hidden clip from a dropped one; synthetic XML and the fixture's structural tests prove the clips are kept. |

| FX → Premiere rectangle as a graphic Shape | A rectangle that is no Color Matte, canvas or guide exports as a graphic of one Shape under the graphic Shape rules: FX's outline (`scene::Path::rounded_rect`, in its f32 arithmetic) from its position to position + size in layer pixels, a positive roundness scaled down by one factor until two radii fit each edge and drawn as four cubic quarter circles with handles 0.5522848 × the radius and the straight edges between them that have a positive length in f32 (FX also draws an edge of no length, or by rounding a few f32 steps backward, as on a pill's sides, where the quarter circles then meet smoothly), which export writes as smooth vertices with tangents (graphic Shapes row): eight, six on a pill and four on a circle (four corners below 1e-6 px); its fill while enabled, with its fill paint: its solid colour, or a linear or radial gradient on the layer's x axis in the gradient row's form, with that row's warnings (opacity stops, a scaled or rotated rectangle, a kept shadow, Opacity below 100, more than three stops); its stroke while enabled, coloured and wider than 0 with its join and miter limit (a stored but disabled fill or stroke is ignored, and so is an undashed stroke's dash offset, as FX draws them); its static Position, Anchor Point, Scale, Horizontal Scale, Rotation and Opacity; a drop shadow and the other layer fields follow the graphic Shapes row. **Approximated** with one warning (`rectangle gradient fill <kind> approximated by its solid fill colour: <reason>; colours differ from the gradient's stops by up to N/255`): a gradient that the Shape rules do not export (reflected or conic, a nonzero y or an axis under 1e-4 px; the reason is theirs) is drawn with the rectangle's solid fill colour, FX's own fallback for it; N, the largest channel difference between a stop and that colour, bounds the error. **Omitted** with the Shape rules' reasons: a paint blend mode, a translucent fill or stroke colour (also under an approximated gradient: FX sets the fill colour to the first stop, so an approximated gradient that fades in from transparent is omitted), dashes, a round join, a bevel join where the outline has a corner (the 90° corners and the ends of a rounded outline's straight edges are in the miter class; a circle has none and keeps a bevel), Motion/Opacity keys ("keyed graphic shapes are unsupported"), masks, a track matte, skew or 3D, and use as another layer's mask or as the path of a text that FX shows; a rectangle that another layer uses as its track matte is omitted with that layer (unsupported Track Matte Key variants row). Keys of the rectangle's size, roundness, fill colour or stroke width leave the static rectangle and are reported ("… animation was omitted"). A rectangle inside a nested sequence exports into the nest by the same rules, as a nested shape layer does (structural only: no native nested graphic is measured). **Before**, every such rectangle was omitted ("rectangle was not exported: …"; a bottommost one, "background rectangle was not exported: …"); **after**, it exports, approximated where stated, or the omission carries the Shape rules' reason. Reimport gives an editable FX shape layer with the same path, paint and transform, not a rectangle: the parametric size and roundness controls, and an approximated gradient, are lost. | Written paths, paints and transforms read back exactly, and the reimport keeps them, the miter join with limit 4 (`rectangles_other_than_color_mattes_export_as_one_shape_or_with_the_shape_reason`, `rectangles_beside_the_mattes_export_as_editable_shapes_and_reimport_as_shape_layers`); the outline equals `scene::Path::rounded_rect` command for command in f32, less the edges without positive length, on eleven cases (clamped to no length and to a step backward, a circle, fractional, below 1e-6 px, negative size). A record whose relative hints are all live but outside the package, without a live alias, still rejects. | **No new loss**: the media is the package-local file, byte-for-byte; nothing is relinked by name or searched for, and the ignored hints are not reported. **Before**, the missing hint stopped the whole conversion; `adobe-modern-speed` now converts its sound (local run, media not committed).
