| FX → Premiere: Eye Contact replacement (`source.eyeContact`) | Enabled: the clip references the active Eye Contact output (`VideoSource::active_asset_id`), packaged byte-for-byte; the original asset and the toggle are lost and reported as an omission. The layer's `sourceIntrinsicDuration` must match the output's duration; a mismatch **rejects** and names the output. Disabled: the original exports and the unused output is reported. Reimport yields a plain video layer of the exported file. Embedded sound of an enabled Eye Contact clip is omitted: the original is not packaged and replacement audio is unverified; other layers keep the audio mapping. Retiming and wipes apply to the active replacement; the existing ramp export approximation is unchanged (synthetic structural interaction tests, not Adobe proof). | Active source choice matches the renderer; bytes are unchanged, not a claim of pixel equality. The lineage and toggle are lost completely. Synthetic mapper tests, including a mismatched output duration, and public export tests; no Adobe reopen. |
| Premiere → FX: nested sequence placement (JRB-1979) | Becomes a `GroupLayer` over the placement's range holding an independent copy of the part of the inner timeline that the placement shows, on the group clock (a retimed placement keeps its inner clock: next row); the placement's Blend Mode is the group's (Opacity Blend Mode row). The inner black canvas is not copied, so inner gaps are transparent; AME renders them transparent too (outer 9–10 s of the fixture shows the red outer clip; AME render of the derived fixture, not a Premiere-saved nest). If Premiere shows black there, every inner gap inside a placement shows the lower outer tracks instead, for the whole gap. At normal speed, inner content outside the placement's window is **not** copied, so lengthening the group later reveals nothing. A retimed nest keeps every inner placement that overlaps its window, untrimmed, and drops only those wholly outside it, so lengthening its mapping can reveal that kept content. Repeated placements become unlinked copies: an edit to one no longer changes the others. The inner sequence survives only as the group name; its GUID and native master/source records are lost. All copies keep the shared media assets. Inner sound, stills, Color Mattes and graphics follow the nests-with-audio row below; embedded video sound inside a nest is reported on export. Other non-video track items are reported by the inner sequence's own read. | Each copied boundary rounds its absolute outer time to the nearest millisecond, as at top level: at most **0.5 ms per boundary**, no accumulation across levels. Synthetic reader/mapper tests plus a structural test of `feature_nested_sequence_strict`, which is **XML-edited** and scored against its AME render (`proof: video_reference`, Asset `eSTtvtw08unRUkVzj8Qs_vid`): a structural derivative, not claimed to have been authored through Premiere's UI; Premiere UI controls were not inspected. Its nest records are edits of `feature_nested_second_sequence_strict`, whose own nest was an edit too, so Premiere has never saved this shape. Neither Adobe-saved corpus nest converts today (`food_lower_third`: nondefault Position; `phone_title`: an effect on the placement). |
| Premiere → FX: unsupported nested placements (JRB-1979) | A nest with reverse playback or a time remap, a speed that its saved window does not confirm, a mixed frame rate or off-frame in/out at normal speed, another canvas size, an out point past the end of its inner sequence, Opacity keys, nondefault Opacity, Motion beside a Track Matte Key, effects, off-frame start/end, or a master clip that plays another source is omitted and reported, never approximated. A forward constant-speed nest whose saved In/Out its speed confirms (Premiere counts inner frames per outer frame) becomes a group whose playback maps the placement linearly onto that window, each end rounded once; its inner placements keep their inner-clock places, a retimed nest inside a normal-speed nest keeps its rate, its Frame Blending/Optical Flow and Motion keys are reported and not converted (static values kept), it has no sound, and export omits the group. So is a placement of a timeline on a nesting cycle (audio-track placements count toward a cycle, so a cycle that runs only through audio also omits the video placements of its timelines: a deliberate over-rejection), or one more than 8 levels deep: each costs that placement, not the timeline. The former expanded-layer and per-read byte quotas are removed (see the capacity inventory). A nest's static or keyed Motion without a Track Matte Key becomes its group's transform and keys, the static value of a keyed property its first key's (Premiere shows that value before a later first key), and the group is clipped to the nested frame by a child guide in the nest-Crop form (formerly deferred by product decision). Media and nest placements are read by one placement reader, so a nest's master marks annotate its source as for media. Child video Motion, Opacity and Crop use the root mapping, with Crop guides kept inside the group; this adds no loss beyond those mappings. Transitions linked to a nest are read and reported like those of any clip. The native writer rejects transition records at every nested depth, as it does at the root. | Rejections convert nothing lossy; synthetic rejection and read-bound tests. A retimed nest's window ends move at most **0.5 ms** each, and its omitted interpolation and Motion keys change the picture unboundedly; synthetic structural and clock tests, no Adobe-native fixture or render. Hidden clips inside a nest become hidden inner layers through JRB-1966's shared mapping, and a hidden nest a hidden group (hidden nest row). |
| Both directions: expanded nested layers (JRB-1979; historical) | **The limits and budget tests in this row are superseded by the quota cleanup; counts and retained bytes no longer omit content.** Historical behavior: `MAX_EXPANDED_LAYERS = 1024` bounds inline expansion separately from the unchanged 256 placements per sequence. On import, tracks are visited bottom first and nests in timeline order; a placement that would exceed the bound is omitted, not the timeline. Its warning names the native item: `nested sequence would bring its timeline to {total} layers, counting its group and every inline copy; at most 1024 are converted`. Export rejects an oversized timeline. Import also enforces a new per-top-level-read budget of 256 MiB of accounted payload (string lengths and vector element sizes, not allocator overhead or resident memory). Placements are copied only after the overlap rule and layer limit keep them. The budget charges each copy and, before they are kept, the items of every sequence being read (a sequence's name and GUID, each media and graphic item, caption cue and caption track style, and each sound). A copy or item that would pass it is an **Occurrence** omission (a caption style omits its **Track**, and a nested sequence whose name and GUID do not fit omits its placement), and its siblings stay: `timeline read limit reached: one top-level read may read and copy at most {bytes} bytes of timeline data`. Charges follow read order and only grow: a failed read or a dropped item keeps its charge. So projects over the budget (many large nested sequences read but not placed, large repeated copies, or large items) lose later placements or items; projects within it convert unchanged. | Measured structural XML expansion of `corporate_slideshow`: 725 layers, resolving placed `VideoSequenceSource` references recursively and counting each group plus inline copies; not a converter diagnostic (unsupported effects and other placement rules reject those placements first). Maximum video nesting depth across 47 staged cases: 3. Synthetic same-asset 256/512/1024-layer exports took 1.57/1.21/1.53 s on the local Mac, each 60 frames at 1080p30 (2 s); startup included, with no superlinear growth observed. This is bounded-load evidence, not an arbitrary-project performance guarantee. The read budget has synthetic reader tests with small injected budgets (`format::reader::nested::tests`, including `shared_text_of_unfinished_reads_is_charged_before_a_nest_descends`, `each_sound_is_admitted_as_it_is_read` and `items_are_charged_for_their_keys`); no large-allocation run and no Adobe evidence. |
| Premiere → FX: retained timeline data (historical reader limit) | **Removed by the quota cleanup; the following describes historical behavior/evidence, not current admission.** One project load keeps at most 256 MiB of accounted timeline data (strings, items, key vectors and every nested copy) across its selected timelines, charged in GUID order before each is kept. A timeline that would pass it is a **Sequence** omission: `retained timeline limit reached: this timeline holds {bytes} bytes of timeline data; {remaining} of the {limit} bytes that one project load keeps remain`. Earlier timelines and later ones that fit are kept; an explicitly selected timeline is charged alone. Projects whose selected timelines together pass the limit now lose the later over-limit timelines. | No lossy conversion. One synthetic `format::reader::load::tests` test with injected limits. Accounted structural bytes, not allocator overhead or resident memory (RSS). With the per-read budget (the items read and the copies made), the timeline data held at once is at most this 256 MiB, one read's 256 MiB and the one item being decoded. The input XML and its record graph, the media records, the track and transition lists of the read in progress and the omission list are outside these budgets, so they do not bound all memory. No Adobe evidence. |
| Both directions: omission list limit (new) | Each conversion's omission list reports an omission once, in first-report order, and holds at most 1024 omissions and 1 MiB (1048576 bytes) of their record and reason text, with room kept for its summary. | FX → Premiere: group as a nested sequence (JRB-1979) | Each group becomes a new inner sequence of its children and a placement from inner time zero over the group's range. Copies of one Premiere sequence become separate inner sequences: re-sharing identical groups as one inner sequence is unsupported (deferred product decision). The group name becomes the sequence name; a group description is dropped and reported. Children past the group's range stay in the inner sequence but are hidden by the placement's out point, as they were in the group. A picture-only group longer than its exported children would place past its inner sequence's end, which is unverified in Premiere, so native lowering **omits and reports the group**, preserving its supported siblings and allowing the ordinary CLI to select its editable AE fallback. A longer group containing retained nested audio still rejects rather than silently losing sound. Native reading/writing still rejects an out point past the inner sequence's end. Its placement carries the group's edits (edited group row) and its blend mode (Opacity Blend Mode row). Other than a stage group, which exports as one clip (clip processing stage row), groups with a track matte, a background (static, or keyed padding or corner radius), animation that the placement cannot carry, a time remap, motion blur, a mask that the F17 check rejects, no name, or more than 8 levels are omitted and reported, and media under them is not inspected. The former 256-placement and 1024-expanded-layer policies are removed; depth, timing, supported-property and cycle checks remain. | Boundaries snap as absolute document times on the sequence grid, so each moves at most **half a frame (16.7 ms at 30 fps)**, as at top level. Motion that moves no pixel stays Premiere's default. A stage wrapper inside the group stays one inner clip. **Before**, every such group was omitted ("group transforms are not supported", "group animation is not supported", "group effects are not supported", "group masks are not supported"). Reimport omits an edited nest (finding F12). | Values and keys are the clip mapping's (**no new loss** over it); a Gaussian Blur exports as the current Gaussian Blur (current Gaussian Blur export row), consistent with FX's blur in layer pixels under uniform Scale (**inferred**; non-uniform Scale averages in FX). Synthetic export tests (Motion, Opacity and keys; Crop, keyed wipe, static, keyed, bypassed and unmapped effects; hidden children) and a native-record test of the G2 document (chain in Index order Opacity, Motion, blur, Crop; unique ObjectIDs; inner timing). **Export passed a scoped Adobe gate** (G2, `export_gate` in `premiere_export_edited_group`; UI not inspected; `structural_only`, not scored): Premiere 26.5.1 reopened the export of an edited group (moved, Scale 70, Rotation 10, Opacity keys 100 to 40, a Gaussian Blur, a Crop guide, two trimmed children) and a hidden group without a warning and read 52 values back as written; AME rendered the placement's scale (0.700) and angle (within 0.01°), its Opacity ramp, frame-exact inner cuts, the Crop before the blur (a blurred Crop edge) and nothing for the hidden nest. The Tesseract render's blurred edge is 15–18 % narrower than AME's (measured; its cause is inferred, not established). The gate wrote the blur as Legacy Gaussian Blur; the current blur, a Linear Wipe, Motion keys and other effects on a placement are not Adobe-verified. Named flips: `an_animated_group_is_omitted_with_its_animation` → `an_edited_group_exports_its_motion_opacity_and_keys_on_its_placement`; the transform, effects and mask rows of `groups_a_nest_cannot_represent_are_omitted_with_their_reason` (the mask row now names canonical_mask's reason); `media_under_an_omitted_group_is_not_inspected` is a table: AnchorPointX keys, ScaleX keys alone and an Opacity cubic between equal values each omit the group with its reason, keep the other group and leave the omitted group's media uninspected. |
| Premiere → FX: inner media at a nest boundary (JRB-1979) | A constant-rate inner media clip, slow, fast or reversed, trims its source bounds in proportion to its authored source and timeline spans and keeps its editable playback (from #4614). Inner media with source-time remapping is omitted with the outer placement, inner clip, track and time range; unit-speed siblings still convert. A Linear Wipe on the nest placement itself omits that placement. A wipe on a unit-speed inner clip uses the shared video mapping, with its guide parented inside the group just like Crop. Still images, Color Mattes and text inside a nested sequence are omitted per item with the same context, and export omits each Image, Text or non-guide Rect layer inside a group; the rest of the nest or group converts (follow-up: inner-sequence writers for those kinds). Effects on an inner clip use the shared per-layer effect mapping in both directions, with effect ids unique across the document; effects on the nest placement itself still omit it. | Structural-only tests check the retime omission and the inner wipe's distinct layer/mask IDs, group parent, mask wiring and reverse mapping, the per-item still/matte/text omissions, and an inner Gaussian Blur imported onto the group child and written in the inner sequence; no new Adobe visual claim. Flat speed/reverse and wipe behavior stays main's. |

### Supported video formats

The accepted set is what the native `tsrct export` renderer decodes and the web
editor can decode through WebCodecs, in formats Adobe documents Premiere
importing. AME has rendered the derived HEVC input; a generated HEVC Premiere
project remains unverified. The existing frame-rate, sequence-canvas and
sourceRect rules apply to both codecs. Every other video file rejects with its
file extension or sample-entry code, in both directions.

| Container | Codec (sample entry) | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| MP4 `.mp4`, QuickTime `.mov` | H.264 `avc1` | Accepted: SPS progressive 8-bit 4:2:0; colour BT.709, unspecified or a pass-through declaration (HDR row above) |
| MP4 `.mp4`, QuickTime `.mov` | HEVC Main or Main 10 `hvc1` | Accepted: `hvcC` Main or Main 10 at 8- or 10-bit 4:2:0 with complete VPS/SPS/PPS arrays, one layer; SPS/VUI progressive, square, limited range, BT.709, unspecified or a pass-through colour; a `dvcC`/`dvvC` box only with a plain HEVC base layer; SPS syntax limited as in [HEVC parameter sets](#hevc-parameter-sets) |
| MP4 `.mp4`, QuickTime `.mov` | Dolby Vision HEVC `dvh1`, `dvhe` | Rejected by name until the engine's sample-entry tables map them (Dolby Vision row above): `Dolby Vision dvh1 sample entries are not decodable by the Tesseract engine; hvc1 with a Dolby Vision record converts` |
| MP4 `.mp4`, QuickTime `.mov` | `avc3`/`hev1` (in-band parameter sets), 12-bit, 4:2:2 or 4:4:4 HEVC, 10-bit H.264, VP9 `vp09`, AV1 `av01`, MPEG-4 Part 2 `mp4v`, Apple ProRes (422 `apco` `apcs` `apcn` `apch`, 4444 `ap4h` `ap4x`, RAW `aprn` `aprh`; see the ProRes row above), APV, DNxHD/HR, Motion JPEG, other | `video codec "<code>" is unsupported` |
| `.m4v` | any | Rejected by name: `tsrct export` finds `.m4v` video, but `AUDIO_EXTENSIONS` in `crates/resource/src/lib.rs` has no `m4v`, so its audio preflight fails every `.m4v` video layer, even a video-only one, as never resolved. Adding `m4v` there lifts this rejection |
| Any other extension (for example Matroska, WebM, AVI, MXF, MPEG-TS) | any | `video file extension "<ext>" is unsupported`; other container content under an accepted name fails MP4 parsing |

Accepted containers still reject fragmented MP4, a second video or audio
stream, subtitle tracks, non-square pixels, full range, colour codes outside the
pass-through table, and clean-aperture cropping; timecode and other data tracks
are ignored. They now also reject an interlaced QuickTime `fiel` box, for H.264 and
HEVC alike; no approved case has one. HEVC rejects every VUI
`frame_field_info_present_flag=1`, a deliberate over-rejection: progressive
streams may set it to carry picture-timing SEI, which conversion does not read.
The browser WebCodecs decoder decodes HEVC only where the browser does.

#### HEVC parameter sets

The VUI checks are only as sound as the SPS fields read before them, so the
reader parses only syntax that a captured encoder stream exercises. Each accepted
branch has a captured `hvcC` record and a full-range twin that differs only in
`video_full_range_flag` (`crates/premiere_file/src/tests/video_format.rs`).
The `hvcC` header must declare Main or Main 10 at 8- or 10-bit 4:2:0, and each SPS must declare the
same chroma format and bit depths, because decoders read them from the SPS. The
SPS is also read for the interlaced-source flag, the decoded size and the VUI,
because the sample-entry `colr` and `pasp` boxes are optional: the in-repo
iPhone-shaped HEVC `tests/rendering-custom/fixtures/ios11361_p3_patches.mov`
has neither and declares Display-P3 primaries only in its VUI, which box-only
checks would accept as BT.709.

| SPS syntax before the VUI | Result | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Sub-layers without sub-layer profile or level fields | Accepted | x265 4.3 `temporal-layers=3` capture and twin |
| Sub-layer profile or level fields | Rejected until a capture exists | x265 and VideoToolbox never write them |
| Scaling lists, with or without `scaling_list_data` | Accepted | x265 `scaling-list=<file>` capture and twin (default, copied, and DPCM matrices, DC coefficients); VideoToolbox enables lists without data |
| PCM (`pcm_enabled_flag`) | Rejected until a capture exists | No local encoder writes it |
| Explicitly coded short-term reference picture sets | Accepted | macOS 26.5.1 VideoToolbox capture and twin: four sets of negative pictures; positive entries use the same read |
| Predicted reference picture sets (`inter_ref_pic_set_prediction_flag`) | Rejected until a capture exists | VideoToolbox codes every set explicitly; x265 codes none in the SPS |
| Long-term reference pictures (`long_term_ref_pics_present_flag`) | Rejected until a capture exists | No local encoder writes them |

The rejection tests set each flag in a captured record at the bit position that
FFmpeg's `trace_headers` reports.

#### Renderer check

The renderer-check numbers come from this comparison. Both sides are relabelled
BT.709 limited range without converting pixels and paired by frame index; frames
1–60 of `ssim.log` are H.264 and 61–120 HEVC.

```sh
F=crates/premiere_file/tests/fixtures
TAGS="format=yuv420p,setparams=color_primaries=bt709:color_trc=bt709:colorspace=bt709:range=tv"
target/debug/tsrct-conv convert $F/feature_video_formats_strict.prproj --to tesseract \
  --sequence c8acf9c1-34b2-4086-9f55-d528950a7059 --output /tmp/formats
target/debug/tsrct export --project /tmp/formats/project.tsrct --output /tmp/formats.mp4
ffmpeg -i /tmp/formats.mp4 -i $F/video-30fps-10s.mp4 -i $F/feature_video_formats_hevc.mp4 \
  -filter_complex "[1:v]trim=2:4,setpts=PTS-STARTPTS,$TAGS[a];[2:v]trim=0:2,setpts=PTS-STARTPTS,$TAGS[b];\
[a][b]concat=n=2:v=1:a=0,setpts=N/30/TB[reference];[0:v]$TAGS,setpts=N/30/TB[export];\
[export][reference]ssim=stats_file=ssim.log" -f null -
```

### Review notes

- Audio placements read their absolute In/Out; audio master-range handling awaits an audio subclip fixture.
- Graphic placements use the same occurrence-level nonzero/invalid `OriginalSubClipTimeOffset` rejection as video and audio (synthetic pinned-text regression); no graphic master-range support is added.
- Eye Contact replacement audio.

## Tests

`tests/cli_smoke.rs` checks arguments, command names, success messages, standard
error, and exit codes by running the executable. Conversion rules,
media validation, publication, and round trips are tested through the public
library API in `crates/premiere_file/tests/conversion`.

From the conv workspace (`opensource/conv` in the enclosing repository):

```sh
make test
make check
make clippy
make fmt
```

The portable workspace contains exactly `tsrct-conv`, `fx_conv`,
`premiere_file`, `aftereffects_file`, `tesseract_file`, `fx_schema`, and
`fx_keyframe_bake`, plus reviewed public dependency patches. The workspace
builds directly without staging, a second repository or private Cargo configuration.

Build-profile parity matters: the existing XML-depth boundary test can overflow
a test thread's stack with unoptimized parser dependencies. The workspace keeps dependency `opt-level = 2`; it does not raise stack
limits or relax the XML depth limit. Passing tests under this declared profile
does not establish safety under every unoptimized build.

For every new conversion feature, follow
the independent fixture and video-proof workflow described below:
add a minimal single-feature Premiere project, an independently exported and
SHA-verified MP4, and a scored FX render. Set `proof: video_reference`
only after the comparison satisfies the standard score policy.
The enclosing repository's `make check-prproj-score` integration target
gates the 31 `video_reference` cases (and fails if
none exist). The conversion-specific PR T4 gate downloads their SHA-pinned
`long_term` Asset videos through the same authenticated resolver used by other
GPU lanes, caching and re-verifying every byte. The
[corpus proof contract](../../tests/README.md#evidence-contract)
lists every gated case. The 38 path-complete real projects in
[`tests`](../../tests/README.md) remain structural
evidence: 37 have immutable Adobe videos for diagnosis, while
`copy_and_paste_effects` has no uploaded video. Numeric Adobe `Sequence.ID` is
accepted; other unsupported production semantics (including missing
`VideoTrackGroup.ComponentOwner`) still block
real-project scores. The legacy offset asymmetric-Scale case, the XML-derived
vertical-canvas case and the 24 fps cut (a one-source-frame lag under every-frame
sampling) are not in the strict gate.

The original unit-test media fixture in `crates/premiere_file/tests/fixtures`
is one second of video-only H.264 at 1080p30. The focused Adobe-oracle fixtures
also include small 10-second colored/timecoded 1080p30 sources. Their QuickTime
MOV source is stream-copy remuxed (`ffmpeg -map 0:v:0 -c copy -f mov`), not a
renamed MP4. A non-square variant exercises rejection (`setsar=2/1`) and an
HDR-tagged one the colour pass-through (BT.2020/PQ declarations); they are
synthetic fixtures, not Adobe references. `feature_hdr_hlg_hvc1.mov` is a
320×180 VideoToolbox Main 10 HLG encode with a timecode track (`-c:v
hevc_videotoolbox -profile:v main10 -pix_fmt p010le -color_primaries bt2020
-color_trc arib-std-b67 -colorspace bt2020nc -tag:v hvc1 -timecode 00:00:00:00
-f mov` on a `setparams`-tagged source).
Tests also mutate container pixel-aspect and edit-list metadata.
Six-frame samples cover the other seven supported rates. Their offline generation
command is recorded in `crates/premiere_file/src/tests/media.rs`. The HEVC Main
and VP9 samples and the captured x265 and VideoToolbox parameter-set variants,
the Main 10 HLG and PQ records among them, are recorded in
`crates/premiere_file/src/tests/video_format.rs`.
